# Decisions — operator rulings, and the levers that are DEAD

**Append-only.** Rows are never rewritten or deleted; a superseded ruling gets a new row
that says so. [`strategy_alpha.md`](strategy_alpha.md) is *what we are trying to see* and
[`engine_reference.md`](engine_reference.md) is *how it is currently seen* — both describe
the present. This file is the only one that remembers what we already tried.

## Why this file exists

Measured on 2026-07-27, during the trend-terminal box gate: four designs were proposed and
falsified in one session. Two of the four were **already documented** in `strategy_alpha.md`
("the root is only a scan origin, not the box's cause"; the back-extension behaviour) and
were still missed, because they sat inside a 1700-line file in a parenthetical. Prose that
is technically present but unfindable does not prevent the bug. A short, boring registry of
*dead things* does.

**Read the Tested-DEAD table before proposing any new knob, threshold, or heuristic.**

---

| 2026-09-08 | **The engine tests against his CURRENT drawings, always. The sealed-snapshot standard is retired.** Operator, verbatim: *"lets make sure that from now on, the engine always Tests against the updated marks as a rule, to check for the latest thing. I thought it was a little bit obvious, no need to hold on to an old drawing if the Operator deleted it or changed it..?"* **He is right, and the state it had produced is the argument:** the sealed Guided List still demanded **three marks he had DELETED** (CTOS 2026-07-23, ORMP 2026-05-08, PKE 2026-02-24) — and two of those were recorded as permanent EXPECTED MISSES, so the engine was being excused forever for failing charts that were no longer ground truth. Meanwhile **five he had drawn since were tested by nothing** (ANRO, MDT, MRK, PKE 2026-04-07, UNF) and **ten of the thirty survivors had been EDITED** — rails, LPS windows, triggers — with the fingerprint able to say only that *something* moved. **The concern I put to him before building, recorded because it is the reason the seal existed:** a standard that silently follows its own ground truth cannot fail, and his own regression signal (EC-29) is *"calibrated-list stocks no longer firing"*. The design answers it by separating the two jobs the seal was doing. **POPULATION is his and follows the DB.** **VERDICTS stay pinned, per drawing** — `marks_corpus.setup_digest` over rails / LPS spans / trigger / knowable-from / frame. An UNCHANGED drawing keeps its verdict, so a pinned hit that stops firing still FAILS and an expected miss that starts firing still FAILS. A REDRAWN or newly DRAWN mark voids its old verdict — it was about a chart that no longer exists — is re-measured, and if the engine cannot read it enters `unreviewed-new-mark`: pinned against silent conversion, reported separately as AWAITING HIS REVIEW, never blocking the rebuild. Drawing a chart must not turn his gate red before he has looked at it. A DELETED mark leaves and its excuse leaves with it. **`--check` now FAILS on a stale corpus instead of printing an advisory** (it had sat drifted for six weeks); an absent marks DB is NOT drift, since CI grades the committed fixture. **Result of the first refresh: the standard went 28/33 → 30/35.** Four of his five new drawings the engine already reads (ANRO, MRK, PKE 04-07, UNF); MDT it does not, and ST 2026-04-17 — whose LPS he moved from March to April — no longer fires under its current window. Those two are the whole review queue. **EC-9 amended and EC-7 narrowed** to keep the half that mattered: he may redraw, an agent may never edit a mark to pass a test, and since a redrawn mark's verdict is VOIDED rather than carried, editing one cannot launder a regression into a pass. Seven new guards, each mutation-proven. | `conventions.md` EC-9 (amended) + EC-7 (narrowed); `tools/guided_list_export.py` (both refusals removed); `tools/marks_corpus.py` `setup_digest` / `population_is_current` / `NEW_MARK_STAGE`; `tests/test_marks_corpus.py`; baseline `tests/baselines/marks_corpus_baseline.json` rebuilt at 30/35 |
| 2026-09-08 | **The first-reaction AR anchor is DELETED — its shape was right and its anchor was not.** Operator, verbatim: *"Lets DELETE the first two."* Ruled a week before the 2026-09-15 kill-by that this flag's ledger row had already declared non-re-datable. `AR_FIRST_REACTION_ENABLED` was dark from 2026-07-04 to deletion, so **nothing the engine reads changed**. **The READING it implemented survives in `strategy_alpha.md`**: the automatic reaction IS the low of the first continuous reaction after the terminal swing — that is still what the AR means, and the retarget's SHAPE was vindicated (spans 1-7 bars against his own 1-5; on HTH its AR landed on his trend end to the bar, exactly as he said: *"the green circle is correct but not for the AR"*). What was deleted is an implementation that could not be right while `climax_bar` is not the trend end. Measured twice against his own dated marks and it lost both times: 2026-08-14 on the cohort chosen to FLATTER it (the twelve largest tighteners in the universe) flag-OFF was closer on 4 of 6, |off| 133 bars vs |on| 266; re-measured 2026-08-31 against the repaired climax polarity it moved FURTHER from flag-off, not closer — touching fewer charts (101 of 335) and disagreeing by more on those (tighten median 19 -> 35 bars) — and on his own marks it is a no-op on 3 of 4 live reads and loses the fourth badly. **The mechanism is the reason deleting costs nothing**: flag-OFF pins `ar_bar` to `box.start_bar`, and his AR IS the box open by his own repeated definition (*"AR at 17/06 which also serves as the root swing for the consolidation"* — IRMD, IART, CYRX), so flag-off satisfies his identity by construction while the retarget broke it to chase a reaction off a climax that is reconstructed backward over a FIXED 60-day window. **That defect stays open as a program** (climax_anchor_diagnosis_2026-08-19) and is the actual work. **Stated cost of this ruling, not hidden:** he was offered the row's own alternative — keep it dark purely as the anchor program's measuring instrument — and chose delete, so the flip half of `tools.operator_marks_diff` retired with it and the program lost a toggle. Its ANCHOR half survives and is now its whole purpose. | `bricks._first_impulse_ar_end` + `market_structure.first_reaction_after` + the flag and its four `AR_*` constants + five `engine_alpha/freeze/manifest.py` rows + `tools/ar_first_reaction_diff.py` all removed; `elected_trend_leg_base` and `analyze.PHASE_A_ANCHOR_FEATURES` deliberately kept; [flag_ledger.md](flag_ledger.md) Retired table; [engine_reference.md](engine_reference.md) "Phase A — First-reaction AR anchor — RETIRED"; [strategy_alpha.md](strategy_alpha.md) "The trend model"; measurement [ar_remeasure_2026-08-31.md](ar_remeasure_2026-08-31.md) + [anchor_marks_ruling_2026-08-14.md](anchor_marks_ruling_2026-08-14.md) |
| 2026-09-08 | **The trend-terminal box gate is DELETED — the doctrine stands, the code does not.** Operator, verbatim: *"Lets DELETE the first two."* Ruled a week before the 2026-09-15 kill-by, which the flag's own ledger row had already declared its **second and last** re-date. `TREND_TERMINAL_BOX_GATE_ENABLED` was dark from the day it was built (2026-07-27) to the day it was removed, so **nothing the engine reads changed** — the deletion is byte-identical by construction, and the only runtime consequence is the manifest rotation. **The RULING it implemented is untouched**: a box may not open before the trend running into it printed its climax (2026-07-27, LIVN, *"way too young to be considered anything but some false breach of the range"*), and the 2026-08-14 corroboration stand append-only above — they rule on the CHART, not on this code. What could not be carried was the implementation's evidence: across three A/Bs (39 → 21 → 17 losses) **the loss cohort turned over completely every time — the intersection of any two of the three loss sets is EMPTY** — so a maturity rule's evidence can never bank and every re-ruling would need a fresh sitting on names nobody has seen; the 2026-07-28 IRMD/IART/CYRX KEEP ruling can no longer be re-tested at all (two are out of universe, the third now reads identical). The defect fingerprint survived the polarity re-key on two disjoint cohorts: class-B losses carry their claimed climax ABOVE the box's own R, 9 of 9 at median +0.45 box heights then 5 of 5 at +0.558 — `segment_trends` box-blindness, not an early box. Recorded honestly against the deletion: 7 of the final 12 class-A losses sit with their climax essentially ON the box ceiling, so the gate was probably right about most of what it removed — which argues the doctrine, not this code. **`market_structure.trend_terminal_floor` SURVIVES untouched and unconditionally live**, feeding Phase A's climax polarity re-key (`bricks._cause_is_up`, 2026-08-19) — the expensive half is kept and its Layer-3b guards MIGRATED to `tests/test_trend_terminal_floor.py` rather than retiring with the flag. | **Overshoot-magnitude test for box legitimacy** (refuse a box whose trend ran N box-heights past R) | **DEAD — falsified 3×** | Anti-correlated with the operator's judgment. He ACCEPTS 47.9% (PXS), 82% (VIK), 101% (MATX) — those are upthrusts *inside* an established base; on PXS he deliberately draws R at 4.66 beneath the 4.92 spike — and REJECTS LIVN at 20.57%. A `TREND_TERMINAL_OVERSHOOT_BOX` knob was built, measured, and removed the same day. The live discriminator is **post-climax maturity** (LIVN 13 bars vs PXS 53, floor `MIN_BASE_DAYS`). | 2026-07-27 |
| **Keying the box's cause trend on `root.kind`** | **DEAD** | The root is a *scan origin*, not the box's cause. LIVN's winning root is an `SC` 420 bars away from the advance that actually swallowed its box, so a kind-keyed gate passed the very defect it was written for — silently. Derive the cause from the segment covering the bar. | 2026-07-27 |
| **Direction-blind trend-segment overlap (later segment wins)** | **DEAD** | Segments share a leg — an uptrend runs to its CHoCH, which IS the next downtrend's start. Letting the later one win lets a base's own automatic reaction (BC→AR) veto the box open at the top where it belongs. Broke 6 pinned Guided-List hits (AVT/CTOS/MATX/NGL/SYRE/VIK). Cause segment (first-write) wins. | 2026-07-27 |
| **Gating a box on its raw `cand_start`** | **DEAD** | Must judge the **back-extended** start, the bar that actually becomes `box.start_bar`. The trend floor is not monotonic: a handover bar carries the OLD (printed) terminal while the next carries the new leg's, so a legal box can sit one bar the far side of an illegal anchor (MATX). | 2026-07-27 |
| **Shelf-R overshoot re-anchoring** | **DEAD — REVERTED** | No clean rule matched the eye; the respect gate kills shelf-R framings on 16 above-rail bars, and that band is also the upthrust defense. Never loosen the respect gate to chase it. | pre-2026-07 |
| **The ATR V-arm check in the Phase-C spring detector** | **DEAD** | Superseded by the bounded-excursion model (penetration → reclaim → hold). Do not re-add. | pre-2026-07 |
| **Two-pass root walk to fix last-resort scope** | **DEAD** | Measured: it reverses CMPR's operator-accepted band election, creating a new violation while fixing another. The per-ROOT `if not pool` guard is pre-existing shared design, not a story/band regression. | 2026-07-27 |
| **Scoring `rs` / `uptrend`** | **DEMOTED to 0** | Edge read 2026-07-22 over 1977 episodes: actively harmful. Merged `fd7fe3b`; S-tier share 51%→29%. | 2026-07-25 |
| **Scoring HTF (weekly/monthly) context** | **DEAD as a score input** | Edge verdict: don't score it. Stays measure-only. | 2026-07-04 |
| **Bar-dwell gate form (EGBN class)** | **DEAD** | EGBN is a confirmed basis artifact, but the gate form breaks 10/26 identities and lets DGII/FLG fire. Merged as measurement only (`d8fd6f6`). | 2026-07 |
| **Multi-ticker `yf.download` batching to cut request count** | **DEAD — refuted at the provider** | Reads like free money (5,488 single-ticker calls → ~110 batched) and the batching code path still exists, so it invites re-proposal. It buys nothing: `yfinance/multi.py` loops **per ticker in both thread modes**, and `base.py` puts the symbol in the URL path — one HTTP request per symbol regardless of batch size. The 2026-07 docstring blaming batching for rate-limit storms is imprecise: what died was `threads=True` (uncontrolled inner threads), not batching. Per-ticker also buys real isolation — one bad symbol cannot taint a batch. | 2026-07-27 |
| **"The cold fetch is slow because it pulls 5 years"** | **DEAD — measured false** | Cold-fetch stages measured 510 s / 499 s against incremental 585–740 s; the *incremental* window is not faster. Request **count**, not payload size, sets the wall clock, and the pool is ~80% idle waiting on rate-limit tokens. Shortening `DOWNLOAD_PERIOD` would cost HTF history and save nothing. | 2026-07-27 |
| **Species-scoped occupancy/traversal floor relaxation** (drop `EQ_MIN_HALF_DWELL`/`EQ_MIN_COVERAGE`, `EQ_MIN_TOUCHES_PER_RAIL`→1, `EQ_MIN_TOUCH_THIRDS`→1, `TRAVERSAL_MIN`→0, `TRAVERSAL_MIN_DENSITY`→0 inside the species read) | **DEAD — measured, operator-approved experiment falsified same day** | The ABVX/MAN autopsies made the floors LOOK like the species wall ("its virtue is its crime"); the full-cache measurement says no: with every floor OFF at clock 8, only **36 of 1,591** refusals flip to episode elections (1,525 hold — the walls behind bind), the flipped cohort **loses money** (fwd_20 median −5.1%, 31% winners, n=35, vs +2.8%/54% for the floors-on elections), **ABVX — the one refusal the operator wanted admitted — STILL does not elect** (its wall is elsewhere: elected-older-box-with-no-LPS + the dragged AR), and the one S4 card that DOES newly elect (ANL) is one he junked. The floors were doing paying work all along. Real levers for the species remain the BREAKOUT WALL (drift-up misfile, S1 6/10 keeps + MAN's recaptured `refused_clock` fact) and the first-reaction AR. Sidecar: `output/power_play_census_relaxed.json` (overrides stamped), vs `output/power_play_census_species.json` clock-8 rows. | 2026-08-18 |
| **A log price scale as the replacement for the vertical proportion trim** (frontend chart framing) | **DEAD — measured worse** | Reads like the obviously right axis for a base sitting on top of an advance: percentages, not dollars, so the rest should get a fairer share of the pane. Measured over the live 230 on identical windows: box share of the visible price range **linear med 0.227 / p10 0.104 vs log med 0.203 / p10 0.085 — log is better on only 18 of 230 charts**. Structural, not incidental: these bases sit at the TOP of the advance, which is exactly where a log axis compresses them against the full visible range. Vertical proportion is bought with a TALLER WELL, never with the axis and never by deleting trading days. Reproduce: `node tools/chart_framing_census.mjs`. | 2026-09-02 |
| **"Just match his TradingView density"** (pin the card to a fixed px-per-trading-day) | **DEAD — measured worse than the shipped model** | The most intuitive proposal in this space. His TradingView reads at ~7.0 px per trading day, so the card should too. In the card's real 687px plot that is a **98-day window: base owns a median 37.8% of width, 88/230 over 45%, 66 over 60%** (5.0 px/day -> 137 days, 27.0%, 65, 38) against the shipped 140-day reference's **26.4%, 17, 3**. He reads at 7 px/day BECAUSE his pane is 1389px wide; copying the density into a half-width pane copies the wrong half. Density is a legibility budget, the SPAN is the invariant. | 2026-09-02 |
| **Putting the band pool's DEPARTURE RUN on the excision-free time axis** (either of the two ways) | **DEAD ×2 — measured the day both were built** | The touch-thirds half of that same axis correction is right and shipped; the run half is not, and BOTH readings of it fail, in opposite directions. **(a) Scatter the judged verdicts back and fill the excised positions False** ("price returned inside"): a 17-day stay below support reads **7** against a cap of 10 and the junk framing is ADMITTED — a permissive rule change with no seam and no ruling. Shipped in `ceb0a27` and removed the same day. **(b) Classify the ORIGINAL window bar by bar so the excursion's own days charge the cap**: reads **17** and refuses that framing, but a qualified event may legally run `BAND_EVENT_MAX_BARS` = 20 bars against `MAX_CONSECUTIVE_OUTSIDE_DAYS` = 10, so it deletes the class it is judging. Measured: **BODI's operator-ruled framing (12.33/10.18, 76-bar window, 30 bars excised) goes from a run of 2 to a run of 19 and stops firing** — the sealed marks ratchet drops 28/33 → 27/33 and `test_near_miss_guards` + `test_story_pool_guards` both go red on it. BODI is the specimen the band flip was accepted on. The rule that stands: the respect gate counts the outside days it OWNS and runs straight across event time (13 on that fixture — refused; 2 on BODI — fires); the excursion's days answer to the event rules instead, which is what "nothing is excused and no gate is weakened" has always meant. | 2026-09-07 |

### Nulls — measured, discriminated nothing

Do not re-run these as defect detectors; they describe the corpus norm, not a defect.

| Measurement | Result |
|---|---|
| Box high above R | 274/332 boxes (82%). Rails are *worked-equilibrium* levels, never climax→AR extremes, so this is design. |
| A later high exceeds the Phase-A climax high | Median +7.5% across the corpus; LIVN sits at the **39th percentile**. |
| Position of the box high within the box | Corpus median 75% depth; LIVN at 43% = 23rd percentile (i.e. *better* than typical). |
| Operator's drawn R vs the window high | His own 33 marks sit a median **+7.7% BELOW** the window high, vs the engine's +3.1% — the opposite of the expected direction. |
| atr_squeeze ADR-rebase (“the open twin”) | Not applicable — measured 2026-08-22: `ATR_10/ATR_50` is already self-relative (the stock's own volatility against its own horizon), so there is no absolute unit left to rebase; corr(score_atr_squeeze, adr_pct) = −0.254 vs the −0.73 that condemned absolute tightness. Recorded so the standing “rebase for consistency” note stops resurfacing. Side fact for the next edge read (not a knob request): 67% of fires sit at ratio > 1 — the term is inert on two-thirds of the fleet. |

| 2026-09-26 | **Build task 8, stage 1 of the rewrite (the swing line and the far view): the builder's defaults, MINE, recorded as mine, and his to overrule at the core sitting.** None of these is his word; each is a choice the stage could not avoid, and each is guarded by a test that was seen red. **(a) One rule for the order inside a bar:** a bar is read in the direction the line came into it, that end first, so a bar entered rising carries its peak and then its valley; a bar commits at most one turn, on its close, and the turn it carries for the new leg is committed by a later bar. **(b) A leg's turn is the most extreme price since the turn before it,** over every bar to the day the reversal was confirmed, the turn bar's own other end included when it was read after the turn. This CORRECTS the copied line in two places the frozen original still has: it restarted the new leg at the confirming bar and so skipped a lower low printed in between (five legs on the 88 fixture charts; on UNF, 08/01/2025, the far view's valley landed a bar late and two dollars high), and a first bar wide enough to open the line on its own was read twice (six of the 88 charts carried the same valley twice). His 130 word-recall rows on his own rails are unchanged by both (THE SOS 23 of 25, Phase C 10 of 10, re-run after the change, byte-identical). The pins that held the copy equal to the original were deleted in the same commit, as the plan says. **(c) The far view is a FOLD of the fine line:** its extremes are the fine line's own committed turns, and a far turn is made when a day backs off that extreme by the far floor in that day's own range, the day's two ends read in the fine line's order. So every far turn is a turn of the fine line and is knowable no earlier than it, by construction (0 of 16,856 far turns outside the fine line at 1.5, 2.5 and 3.0 on the 88 charts, none knowable early). Two other constructions were built and rejected: the same bar rule in the far view's own direction falls out of phase on every wide bar (1,064 far turns on bars the fine line has no turn on at 1.5 ranges, about twelve a chart), and the bar rule in the fine line's order is nested on the fixtures but not by construction (a day whose range jumps can print a higher high the fine line never turns on, and the blind check built one). Its floor is a parameter; the listing gate places it. **(d) A turn's size** is the leg into it, in the daily range of the day the turn printed; the opening turn has none. **(e) A turn's knowable day** is the close that confirmed it; the opening turn's is the day its first leg covered the floor, even on the chart's first bar (a turn by its shape, whose KIND is known only then; the copy stamped bar 0). **(f) The daily range is built on the whole history before the two-year cut** (the old reader cuts first and computes a cold unit inside the window); the window is the old reader's own (`2y`, copied), so the yardstick compares like with like; the smoothing is the old ATR_10's ten days. **(g) What the left edge may change:** the line forgets where its left edge fell once it has found its first shared turn: measured on the 88 charts, at worst 28 bars after a 20-day trim (TNC, a stretch where every bar covered the floor twice over and the two lines read them in opposite order), median 1; the far views at worst 20, 69 and 69 bars at 1.5, 2.5 and 3.0 ranges (medians 4, 9.5 and 11). One Simple, Smart, Adaptable method to reach as many high quality setups as possible."* His go-ahead (Sun 13/09/2026), verbatim: *"Go! I get it when we get into specifics it become tricky but I hope we can develop a smart/adaptable way to catch as many good setups as possible"*. **The whole-engine inventory that preceded it** (eight readers, 16,419 lines, 337 decision points: 101 thresholds, 85 gates, 63 flags, 45 elections, 28 rescues, 15 vetoes) found one structural inversion everywhere: the box is elected by gates BEFORE any event is read, the LPS is a brick of that election, the event map runs after the fire as diagnostics, and the grade puts 163 of its 171 points on box statistics. **The method as ruled, one pass per stock per read day:** (1) one threshold line on the whole chart, 0.75 daily ranges wick to wick, the first and last bar turns by their shape, named events turns by law; runs of higher highs and higher lows are trends, the climax is the run's highest turn, the automatic reaction the first valley after it; (2) the rails: candidate pairs walked forward from the climax (the climax and its reaction first, else one swing right), the root is the first pair whose two rails each receive a later turn within the rail area, tightest only breaks a same-day tie, the box opens on the earlier anchor and never extends left, its unit is the election day's ATR frozen with the rails, 15 trading days is the age floor; (3) the events, forward in time against the rails: turns at a rail, one Phase C per box by the swing-recovery rule, every thrust over resistance typed by the swing after it under ONE precedence table (LPS above R, upthrust, SOS, child candidate, open), shakeouts, mini consolidations with their own rails, Phase D opening at the first right-side evidence, the LPS window as the last run of receding days with the day before it as its top, the trigger the last day's high, the buy its cross; (4) the grade: tightness (height in ranges, spread profile), the event map, context, all graded, a named event's swing never docked; (5) the display: one state word per chart. **The yardstick for every step is concordance, never rail replication**, and his Sun 13/09/2026 words make the LPS the judge of a pair: *"some Earlier & Wider Variations can be acceptable as long as they follow and understand most of my Idea's within that mark, especially the LPS"*. **The build order, one flag each, measured alone against his 35 marks and the 18 junk charts:** 1 the guards (the junk and fleet fixtures grade the fired-policy window like the marks ratchet; the junk population re-frozen with NVT out on his Thu 10/09/2026 word), nothing in the engine moving; 2 the ruled stack R7 to R18 behind flags plus one window per read day; 3 LPS refusals to grades; 4 the one line feeding the event map and the trend labels only; 5 the words on the line; 6 the 15-day floor from the first anchor; 7 box gates to grades (width caps, crash floors and the spring cap, then respect share and the run cap); 8 the LPS leaves the box election, the state table on the wire; 9 the box opens on the earlier anchor with the back-extension deleted; 10 the climax painted back from the elected pair and root candidates as one ordered list; 11 the hand-over by swings, then the stale-box rescue retires; 12 the grade re-partition with the Power Play lane deletion. | 2026-09-13 | **THE LINE: one zigzag on the whole chart at one sensitivity, 0.75 daily ranges wick to wick, edge bars as turns, no retracement ratio.** Asked for the smallest counter-move that counts as a swing on his line, he asked for a search, verbatim: *"could you help me with that one? can you conduct a search, even within archived setups to look for for an answer for the possible smallest counter moves, its hard to tell, it depends on the gross move over it can be one big bar or a collection of them."* On the first and last bar as turns: *"Yes even with out "Future" Information we can deduct them as turns by their current state swing shape"*. **Measured (his 35 marks, 94 drawn events, the archive's 11,618 fires):** his 37 LPS pullbacks run 0.88 to 2.33 daily ranges (median 1.27), retrace 0.26 to 1.14 of the leg they counter (median 0.45), 16 of 37 are one bar's move; his smallest named counter-move anywhere is VIK's LPS at 0.88. A threshold line scored on his 186 named turns (both anchors, every LPS peak and low, every spring and test tip, every SOS peak): floor 0.5 with the first and last bar allowed 183 of 186 (7.2 extra turns per 10 trading days); **0.75: 180 of 186 (6.0)**, landing on every LPS peak and low, every SOS peak, every spring tip, every R anchor, the six misses being S anchors that share the climax's bar; 1.0: 174 (loses RGR's and VIK's LPS); 1.5: 107; **today's skeleton (order 1, order 2 at 40 trading days, the 15-percent-of-box collapse): 112 of 186**, because an order-2 pivot needs two lower bars on each side and 25 of his 37 pullbacks bottom within two days, and the edge mask hides the first and last bars. A retracement ratio (a fifth, a third, a half of the leg, over a 0.5 or 1.0 floor) recalls exactly what its floor alone recalls: his legs run 1.5 to 5.3 ranges, so the floor always dominates (Tested-DEAD row). On the 18 junk charts turn density is the same as his (8.5 vs 8.1 per 10 trading days) but the rail share differs (58 percent of his turns within 0.25 ranges of a rail; 24 to 29 percent on the tall junk boxes): a graded trait, not a floor. The archive's own LPS counter-move has median 1.13 ranges, his six liked fires 0.55 to 0.94. **Ruled into the build:** one line, one floor of 0.75, the first and last bar as turns, an outside bar may carry both turns, named events (the LPS valley, the trigger cross) are turns by law, the retrace share archived per swing; the line is NOT the root swing (his anchor pair is a consecutive line swing on 21 of 35) and NOT the LPS window (a sub-floor bounce sits inside 25 of his 37 windows). Once a box is elected its rail area, hand-over test and ceiling use the election day's ATR frozen with the rails. | [rulings_ledger_2026-09.md](rulings_ledger_2026-09.md) fifteenth sitting (swing_sweep); [final_method_2026-09.md](final_method_2026-09.md) §1, §2 |
| 2026-09-13 | **THE RAILS AND THE ROOT: the climax is the trend's end, the root swing is the first pair that wraps the respected range, first in time wins, the box opens on the anchors, respect and height and depth are graded, fifteen trading days is the one floor.** The climax, verbatim: *"that extreme is the Buying/Selling climax and the counter move after is what's known as the automatic rally. Since the price graph has only 2 states either Trending or Consolidating this question is easier to answer even if there isn't a clear cut answer for an extreme running end, I just look for the transition between the Trend and the first sensible structure that resembles a consolidation"*. The root swing, verbatim: *"as noted before the Root swing can be the BC/SC -> AR, but in plenty of bases and consolidations it ISN'T and the ROOT SWING is the first Set of limbs(Swings) that their anchors answer to the true trading range of said consolidation -> so not the first swing after said climax but we look for the first place in the timeline (after the climax obviously) where drawing the anchors for Resistance and Support lines Wrap around the Consolidation trading range that respects it. - ngl is a nice example"*; and the walk: *"Option A: Trend -> BC/AR = Root Swing -> Consolidation Box. Option B: Trend -> BC/AR -> RootSwing -> Consolidation Box."* The box opens: *"No, Box opens on the anchors of each of the Boundary rail (Resistance & Support)."* Respect by context: *"a whole lone bar beyond the rail area isn't respecting it but if we come to learn that price action before that bar and after that bar DO then it changes the way we treat it, a Long run of bars beyond the rail could mean a long Spring or UP thrust as well, if the price continues to Rise/Fall with out recovering we can deduce that either that the consolidating structure we measured ended and the price began to trend ... Context is key"*. Height: *"a box is a box because of its consolidating Zig zag behavior not it's height but generally Since we prioritize tight Boxes we generally prefer to avoid HUGE boxes"*. Depth: *"It's hard to gate using a raw number in case we reject a valid setups because of a small neumeric gap"*. Length: *"Lets go with 15 days"*, then *"15 for a base minimum yes."* On the earlier, wider pair the forward walk elects on 9 to 11 of his marks: *"nothing really disqualifies them (not until I've seen it ofc) ... for the engine's sake it's probably better to keep it that way so we won't have the problem of tuning the meaning of the word "correct" or the "tightest" being some really far pair that cuts the consolidation length in half"*. **Measured:** his anchor pair is not a consecutive swing on the 0.75 line on 14 of 35 (nine carry a 0.95 to 1.6-range bounce between the anchors, five anchor S on the climax bar with no counter-move); the rule that reproduces eight of the nine: the S anchor is the lowest low of the reaction before the first higher low. Every cheap "answered to" test elects an earlier, wider pair on 9 to 11 marks (ALB, AVT, BWA, JAZZ, NTCT, WTS, YPF, RGR, SILC, both NGL); he accepts that at the cost of the LPS judging it. The eleven boxes the engine opens before his climax today are older roots electing other anchor pairs (MRK 89 trading days early, UNF 53, ALB 34), not the back-extension (0 days on ten of them). At his rails the whole-bar respect share is 0.79 to 1.00 and every run beyond a rail that resolves inside his span re-engages within 1 to 7 trading days; the long runs are all events he drew. His heights run 0.98 to 3.13 ranges, the junk fires 1.3 to 4.9; the percent-of-price caps refuse BODI (2.1 ranges, 21 percent of price) and demote ANRO; the archive's tail falls almost monotonically with height. BODI's drawn Phase C (3.3 ranges under S, 17 trading days) is refused three ways today (the 3-range cap, the crash floor, the width cap). The run into his climax: min 3.2, median 7.4 ranges over 35 trading days. **Ruled into the build:** candidate pairs walked forward from the climax, first in time wins, tightest only a same-day tie-break; the box opens on the earlier anchor, each rail from its own anchor, never extended left, no drawn end; respect refuses nothing, every run beyond a rail typed by the swing after its tip; no height gate, height the largest graded term; no depth number, a dip beyond the support area is a Phase C candidate until the swing rule names it a spring or a breakdown; 15 trading days counted from the first anchor as the one floor. | [rulings_ledger_2026-09.md](rulings_ledger_2026-09.md) fifteenth + sixteenth sittings; [final_method_2026-09.md](final_method_2026-09.md) §3 to §9 and "Sixteenth sitting" 1, 2 |
| 2026-09-13 | **THE EVENT MAP: upthrust and shakeout join the vocabulary, the SOS never gates and is typed by what follows, Phase D opens at the first right-side evidence, the mini consolidation has its own rails, recovery is judged by the swing, the last supper is just another event, one Phase C per box.** Verbatim: *"we can add Up Thrust, Shake Out as I use them on a daily basis."* The SOS: *"an SOS will never gate a setup firing, It's a sign of strength and that definition is easy to decipher, it dosen't have to be exactly from the support line and it dosen't have to break through Resistance it's just that the easiest and cleanest examples do, but Since I don't want you highlighting every piece of running buyers it most be in Phase D (The Right Most Area in the consolidation after the 'V' tip (either by after Phase C) or Price Contracting behavior which SOS is one of)) and it's Commonly spotted after some sort of Shakeout Like move and it reaches or breaches local Significant Highs like the Resistance."* Phase D: *"Phase D can open at the SOS if it's the first sign of Price action supporting the fact that we are in the Right side of the 'v'. at the worst Phase D starts at the LPS (as a fail safe) but most cases that isn't going to happen, generally phase D needs to start after the middle of the structure"*; and on his two examples: *"On Syre I would say Phase D opens roughly after the 26/01/26 because its after the spring and after the price recovers and its a bunch of few bars before the SOS and The LPS. and on PBT I would say it starts on 23/04/26 because price contracts (bar Spread is lower which means the Price Moves less) Closer to Resistance (Meaning gradual Support rise like a stair case) before a Thrust and a final pull back"*. The mini consolidation: *"The Mini consolidation is always built with it's own rail set - the LPS read can be against it if it is inside/near it/resting on it and the trigger is always read against the LPS by itself."* Recovery: *"Recovery better be determined by the Swing rather then a singular bar ... we need to extend recovery to bars that are also near the support area as well like Semi support breaching bars (Poking through) and ones that touch support from below as well, but since we don't want to invite Junk I think its better to be kept by the swings for added context."* The last supper: *"It's just another event that happened, lets not dwell on it too much"*. Phase C: *"A shake out is also a dip I would probably name, but we can stick with Phase C only for now, and a Dip inside the support area will probably not ever qualify since that describes more of the basic Chop common pattern we are used to see."* The pullback above resistance: *"this can be all 3 depending on what comes next. in the case of ANRO its an LPS above Resistance that fires. in other cases if that "Rest" above R evolves to be a mini consolidation then it can be considered for a candidate for that mini consolidation's root swing ... our engine can detect pull back "LPS" like behavior across the entire base and earlier points for an LPS can be valid and be pointed out ... we returned to the original intent of highlighting a single LPS per stock, the freshest and most relevant one"*. **Measured:** on his 35 boxes the line prints 136 up-swings over the resistance area (24 he named SOS, 12 inside an LPS, 5 inside a last supper, the rest unnamed); no rule read at the peak separates them, only the swing after; five separate card rules named 61 of his own valleys three ways, hence ONE precedence table: the down-swing recedes with the trigger overhead and its low holds R minus 0.5, an LPS above R, fires; the next valley takes out the thrust's launch low by more than 0.5 ranges before any LPS, an upthrust; otherwise an SOS with "last supper" as hindsight data; "child candidate" a flag confirmed only by a later turn at the child's anchors; at the right edge, open. The engine today names his SOS on 2 of 21 (its typer demands a held wave inside a hold band) and opens Phase D at the deepest valley, median 0.39 of his boxes, where his first right-side event sits at median 0.79. **The Phase D rule placed from his two dates and measured on all 35:** opens at the earliest of the round trip after the spring (the recovery swing reaches the resistance area, then the first valley that holds in the support area: SYRE Mon 26/01/2026 exactly), a staircase of rising swing lows in the upper half with the peak between them at resistance, the first SOS, the first LPS; median position 0.74, after the middle on 31 of 35, never before a third of the box; PBT lands Tue 28/04/2026 against his Fri 23/04 (his staircase there is bar-level inside one swing, and the bar-level form opens Phase D inside Phase B on 11 marks: Tested-DEAD row). His ten Phase Cs recover by the swing in 2 to 9 trading days (median 4); his five minis are 12 to 42 trading days and 0.70 to 1.79 ranges tall, the LPS low inside the band on 4 of 5; his ten last suppers dig 1.75 to 3.76 ranges in 1 to 4 days off tops 0.6 to 2.4 ranges over R. **Ruled into the build:** the two words; the SOS as an up-swing launching at or after the box's deepest low, covering at least his minimum in ranges (1.70), top at the resistance area or above the last swing high, provisional at the edge, THE SOS being the thrust whose pullback is the LPS; Phase D by the four openings, "after the middle" graded; the mini as a second rail set written as one event on the parent's map, the LPS elected against the parent and displayed against the mini when inside or within 0.5 ranges of it, the trigger always the LPS's high, a band whose bottom holds the R area being a child instead; recovery = the up-swing from the tip reaches the support area and the next down-swing makes a higher low, failed on a lower low before any thrust; one Phase C per box, the deepest recovered, a dip inside the area a turn at S; the last supper named in hindsight only, never weighted; every LPS-like pullback an event, one highlighted. | [rulings_ledger_2026-09.md](rulings_ledger_2026-09.md) fifteenth + sixteenth sittings; [final_method_2026-09.md](final_method_2026-09.md) §10 to §15 and "Sixteenth sitting" 3, 5, 12 |
| 2026-09-13 | **THE LPS AND THE TRIGGER: one window per read day, the last run of receding days with the day before it as its top; a cross inside the pullback is a buy; the LPS is ON support when its bodies sit above it; no post-window fatal; volume is data.** Verbatim, on SYRE's two windows ending the same day: *"10/02 to 11/02"*; on the start: *"Lets go with the day after"*; on the support side: *"Lets say not below the support area (the area below the support that can be considered respected)"*, then on JAZZ: *"I would say the LPS is on the Support rather then Under it since most of the move is above it and only small parts of it poke out down"*; on the fatal character change: *"idk"*; on volume: *"lets go with data only?"* and *"Can't say nailing down volume to points is tricky, we generally want to see Low Volume at key points Like Phase C and LPS, but a high Volume at Phase C can also mean that spring was a successful choke point of sellers ... Today's code elects the LONGER of two windows sharing an end (the opposite of his answer) and refuses one-day windows (five of his are one receding day). JAZZ's window: every close above S, every high 0.5 to 0.8 ranges above S, only the lows poke under (to 0.74): "on support" is typed by the window's bars, never its lowest wick. Under R18 plus the trigger law no post-window day exists to test (every later day either crosses the last day's high or recedes into the window), so the three post-window checks (a close 5 percent of price under the LPS low, which is 0.5 ranges on BODI and 2.3 on UNF; the low test; the spread test) have nothing to do; 11 of his 37 windows sit at or over today's volume ask of 0.87 and BWA's only wall was volume. **Ruled into the build:** one window per read day, no election; the top defined on the line as the last turn before the window low; one-day windows legal; a cross inside the window is a fire kept with the story position "crossed inside the pullback", the window continuing and firing again on its last receding day; the low's position graded with no support-side refusal; the zone typed by the window's bars; the three post-window checks deleted, depth from the top and length graded (steep past 5 receding days, his max); volume never refuses, never elects, carries no points, is shown as a number. | [rulings_ledger_2026-09.md](rulings_ledger_2026-09.md) fifteenth + sixteenth sittings; [final_method_2026-09.md](final_method_2026-09.md) §16 to §20 and "Sixteenth sitting" 4, 7, 8, 10 |

His words made exact: a named event's swing is never docked, an unnamed lunge beyond a rail is. **Measured:** base length does not separate the tail (rank correlation 0.04 with a 25 percent move in 20 days); the buy is the day whose high crosses the trigger on 37 of 37 but the close ends back under it on 12 of 37, and the engine's still-live test reads the close (default: read the high, so "nothing" holds on every buy day); the door's "yearly leg" is the 12-month return floor (a stock down more than 20 percent over the last year is not scanned), refusing the same population as the 200-day leg (MDT on his marks; MAN's June box on Wed 03/06/2026 in the field), so both stay together; the premise put to him that R18 stopped MAN was FALSE: the fleet's Fri 05/06/2026 MAN fire was a 140-day box refused on its last receding day by the 200-day leg, and his named Power Play MAN is the Jul/Aug 2026 shelf (19 trading days) that never fired in the paying read; HESM's lost window was five days of rising highs and lows, a rally, not an LPS; a close a full daily range over R sits inside 10 of the 35 boxes he kept as ONE before their final LPS, so a close-dated hand-over would freeze SYRE's parent on Fri 13/02/2026 before his second LPS and lose the fire (Tested-DEAD row); the swing rule (after the breakout, the first swing whose valley holds the parent's R area is the child's root candidate, confirmed by a later turn at a child anchor) finds NGL's root Mon 09/02 to Wed 11/02/2026 to the day and MRK's Tue 30/06 to Wed 01/07/2026 with its valley 0.13 ranges under the old R. **Ruled into the build:** the grade reads height in ranges, the spread profile inside the box and the window, and the event map; base age zero weight, archived; turns per rail replace touch bars; the named-leg rule replaces the blanket pardon; one grade ledger with a fate for every one of today's 171 points is written before the flip. A fire is box plus LPS plus trigger; the LPS leaves the box election; one state word per chart (fired, crossed, lines with no LPS yet, forming N of 15, root candidate unconfirmed, beyond R undetermined, under S undetermined, broke down, not scanned, no lines), the no-LPS charts in a watch lane. Nothing on the board on the buy day; the ticker page may print "fired dd/mm, crossed dd/mm" from the archive. The door stays outside the four stages with its refusal named on the ticker page. One walk, one map, one clock of 15 trading days; the species lane and its 8-day clock retire; the pole is a trend fact on the map. The parent stays operative until the child is confirmed by a turn, then freezes back to its breakout day and the child inherits it as its cause; the close is used once, for the breakout day (R15). | [rulings_ledger_2026-09.md](rulings_ledger_2026-09.md) fifteenth + sixteenth sittings; [final_method_2026-09.md](final_method_2026-09.md) §21 to §26 and "Sixteenth sitting" 6, 9, 11 |

| 2026-09-10 | **R10 to R16, the second to seventh sittings: the root swing anchors the rails, a box hands over after its breakout, COLM is junk, the LPS stays close to the box (ceiling 1.35 ranges), the bar is the unit of dwell, the date convention, the close is for the breakout day only.** R10, verbatim: *"Yes, I always described the Root Swing as the Swing that Anchors the Rails of the consolidation you can even search that in the damn chats, and the elected Phase A should consists of Two parts yeah? the Climax and the automatic rally"*, later *"Correct that's why its weird it didn't go by that name because I authored it long ago, it works like that already."* R11: *"Regarding EWTX I can see why a box was elected there, which could may have counted as a setup, but after that 'setup' you marked breached out another consolidation was formed right after, And I need to know our engine can recognize that"*, and as a story step: *"Trend -> Climax -> Consolidation (+ whatever traits that consolidation holds) -> Break out -> (That's the second Box that I drew) Trend -> Climax -> Consolidation"*. R15: *"Yes Close Value is the right tool to measure that, but the Pull back itself is to be measured by High and Low values, like always."* (the breakout day is read on the close; every measure of the pullback reads highs and lows; the placed close floor re-placed on the last bar's high, identical on every count). NGL, his drawing: *"Red marked stripes are Root Swings, LS is Last supper"* (two boxes, the second's BC and AR overshooting both rails and the root swing coming after them: Phase A and the root swing are two things). **Measured:** today's painted Phase A equals the rail-anchoring swing on 0 of 30 (polarity wrong on 16 of 30); the hand-over in ranges at 1.5 gave PBT its exact box and EWTX its Jan box at no fleet cost; the ceiling 1.35 costs DGII (junk) and gains ST and NOK. | [rulings_ledger_2026-09.md](rulings_ledger_2026-09.md) second to seventh sittings of 2026-09-10 (rulings_ab2 to ab5, his_dwell_bars, pullback_bar_floor, syre_facts) |

| 2026-09-07 | **The scan-failure report is cut to TWO states, named for the ACTION, not the cause.** Operator, on reading the feature description: *"I don't need that much of a detailed report, I just need to know 2 states either the Scan failed because of a technical issue (meaning, I need to run the scan manually and everything works), or there is a real issue that needs to be tended by you!"*. He is a discretionary trader glancing at a dashboard, and the only decision the surface has to support is **do I click re-scan, or do I ping Claude**. So the headline is now **Re-run the scan** (`rerunnable`) or **Needs attention** (`needs_attention`), and the taxonomy, the reason and the proposed solution are **kept — demoted to the second line**, not deleted; he said he did not need the detail UP FRONT, not that it should not exist. **Re-runnable** covers every interruption kind (shutdown / service-only / unrecorded / not-yet-known) and an aborted run: the machinery is fine, the run just did not reach the end. **Needs attention** covers a deliberate engine refusal (`stale_data` — running the same scan again meets the same refusal), a program error / non-zero exit, and **anything unrecognised, which fails CLOSED**: two live rows carry a string whose writer no longer exists, and we cannot promise a re-run fixes what we cannot explain. **The escalation is the part that earns its keep: the same failure on three consecutive runs of one job (`REPEAT_ESCALATION = 3`, server-side) stops being re-runnable**, because someone re-running the same broken thing nightly is the exact trap this feature exists to prevent — one night is noise, two can be two nights of his habitual power-off, three is a pattern. The streak is keyed on (job, status, resolved failure kind): a different failure breaks it, another job's run in between does not, and it can only ever HARDEN a verdict. **Consequence worth recording:** the health pill and `/scan-status/latest` now read a short WINDOW of recent runs rather than one row — with one row the pill would say "re-run it" about the very run the registry three inches below had already escalated. **EC-28 throughout:** the verdict is resolved in `scan_diagnosis` (one mapping table, a coverage test that fails when a stampable kind has no row), `/health` serializes it as `verdict` alongside a per-check verdict on `failing`, and the frontend holds only the two labels and their tones in `wireVocabulary.js` — a grep guard fails if either slug appears in a rendering module, which is what a JS-side derivation would look like. Every guard mutation-proved (22 mutations, each watched red, each restored). | `services/scan_diagnosis.py` (`RERUNNABLE`/`NEEDS_ATTENTION`, `KIND_VERDICTS`/`STATUS_VERDICTS`/`DEFAULT_VERDICT`, `REPEAT_ESCALATION`, `_escalate_repeat`, `overall_verdict`); `services/health.py` (`verdict` on the report and on each failing check); `/scan-status/latest`; `components/wireVocabulary.js` `RUN_VERDICT_LABELS`/`RUN_VERDICT_TONES`; `utils/appFormat.js` `buildHealthPill`; `components/ScanHistoryModal.jsx`; **evidence: [scan_interruption_incident_2026-09.md](scan_interruption_incident_2026-09.md)** "The operator cut the report to TWO states"; `tests/test_scan_diagnosis.py`, `tests/test_backend_services.py`, `utils/appFormat.test.js`, `components/wireVocabulary.test.js` |
| 2026-09-07 | **Review of the scan-diagnostics change: the shutdown claim was anchored on the wrong moment, and the health pill described three healthy states as a failure. SUPERSEDES the window stated in the row below.** Three independent reviewers returned ship_after_fixes. (a) **The window `[started_at, min(detected_at, started_at+2h)+90s]` swallows this operator's habit.** A reviewer read the real System log: all eight recent `User32` 1074s (2026-08-30 .. 09-06, 21:59Z-23:58Z) fall inside the 18:00 scan's own two-hour cap, so an orphan detected late — the dashboard survives, `finished_at` is stamped at next morning's boot — was stamped *"the computer was shut down while the scan was still running"* whatever actually killed it, with a remedy that would not have helped. The claim is now anchored on **`detected_at`**, the one instant we can PROVE the run was still alive (the reconcile finding the row still 'running'): a 1074 must sit within the 90 s detection grace of it, keeping the existing cap. The real incident still classifies (the 1074 landed 4.95 s before the reconcile); the late-detection case drops to `interrupted_unrecorded`. (b) **The `last_scan` health check said "The most recent scan did not finish cleanly" in three states where the scan finished perfectly** — never-run, hung-running, and ok-but-stale. The third IS the morning after this incident: the machine was off, so no scan ran, and the newest row is yesterday's clean scan aged past its allowance — the interface described a run that wrote 12 setups as not finishing cleanly. Resolved per state from one server-side map. (c) **`interrupted_power_loss` DROPPED, not fixed.** Windows stamps EventLog 6008 and Kernel-Power 41 at the NEXT boot, so their timestamp is the reboot, not the crash, and an overnight power cut lands hours outside any run-anchored window: the arm was near-dead and its absence is covered honestly by `interrupted_unrecorded`, now softened to *"Windows has no record of the computer going down while it was running"*. (d) **The `wevtutil` non-zero-exit refusal had NO test** — a reviewer deleted it and all 1821 tests stayed green, because `parse_events` returns `[]` for empty stdout AND for plain error text, so that one branch is the whole difference between "could not read the log" and "read it, found nothing". Now pinned and mutation-proved. (e) EC-3 folds: `HUNG_RUNNING_HOURS` had three homes (diagnosis / watchdog / a bare `2` in health) and is now one imported constant; "which checks count as an operator-facing failure" was written twice in `health.py` and is now one predicate. (f) EC-28: `/health` now serializes `failing` — which checks are wrong, in order, in plain words — and the frontend's `failingChecks` is a pass-through instead of a second membership rule in JS. (g) The remedy prose no longer hardcodes "18:00": the hour is a setting he is being asked to move, and only `missed_slot_notice` (which reads it live) may name it. (h) Non-scan rows get their own noun and their own re-run instruction, so an outcome-backfill failure no longer reads "the scan" with a "press Evaluate" remedy. | `services/scan_diagnosis.py` (`classify_interrupted`, `JOB_NAMES`/`JOB_RERUN`, `_LAST_SCAN_STATES`); `services/health.py` (`_operator_relevant_failures`, `failing` on the wire); `services/scan_watchdog.py`; `utils/appFormat.js`; `components/wireVocabulary.js`; **evidence: [scan_interruption_incident_2026-09.md](scan_interruption_incident_2026-09.md)**; `tests/test_scan_diagnosis.py`, `tests/test_backend_services.py` |
| 2026-09-07 | **A scan killed by the nightly shutdown must SAY SO, and the "Degraded" pill must click through to a diagnostics registry.** Operator: *"Well if the Run was ended because the computer was offline, just make it say that, or If I click on the 'Degraded' area it just takes me to a log registry with the proposed solution and reason of failure"*. Both halves shipped. **The load-bearing finding is that the obvious implementation is FALSIFIED, and it is recorded here so it is not re-proposed.** The intuitive rule — at boot-reconcile time compare the orphaned run's `started_at` against the machine's last boot — returns **False for the exact incident**: NSSM restarted uvicorn *inside* the dying Windows session, so the reconcile ran at 01:00:30.464 local, five seconds after Shut Down was pressed and one second AFTER the Event Log service had stopped; the machine's last boot at that instant was the previous MORNING. It also lies in the other direction (a scan that crashes alone at 01:00 on a machine that stays up, rebooted normally at 09:00, satisfies `boot > started_at`). **What shipped instead is a two-moment design.** The boot reconcile RECORDS A FACT — plain-words error text plus `failure_kind='interrupted_unknown'` — and claims no cause, because at that instant the cause is provably unreadable. `scan_diagnosis.resolve_pending`, run off the event loop at lifespan startup when the Event Log service is actually up, reads the Windows System log for the run's OWN bounded window and stamps a TERMINAL kind ONCE. **The kind is stamped, the prose is derived at the publisher** — so wording is a code change with no migration, and (the reason for the column) a **circular 20 MB System log that rolls cannot rewrite a historical answer**. The offline claim is affirmatively qualified: a `User32` 1074 inside `[started_at, min(detected_at, started_at+2h)+90s]`. A readable-but-empty log is a DIFFERENT value from an unreadable one, and "the computer stayed on" is asserted only when the boot clock also shows no restart since the run began — the clock may only REFUSE, never grant. **Both topbar pills** now open one registry (the failure text has always lived on the second one, so wiring only "Degraded" would have been the wrong half); it leads with the failing health checks, carries a synthetic line for the night the scan never STARTED (2026-09-06: powered off 30 s before the slot, no row at all, and `_missed_todays_slot` structurally cannot catch it up), and lists all three job kinds — surfacing 11 non-ok maturation/download rows no surface had ever shown. **Cosmetic bug fixed in the same change:** the topbar rendered an interrupted run's NULL setup count as "0 setups" (`Number(null)` is zero and zero is finite) and the operator read it as "the scan ran and found nothing" — the file had re-declared the house null guard instead of importing `finiteOrNull`, exactly the drift the null-safety rule exists to stop. | `services/scan_diagnosis.py` (the one classifier); `scan_runs.failure_kind` + its backfill; `startup._reconcile_orphaned_runs`; `scan_status.never_run`; `/scan-status/history?kind=all`; `components/ScanHistoryModal.jsx` + `hooks/useScanHistory.js`; `utils/appFormat.js`; **evidence: [scan_interruption_incident_2026-09.md](scan_interruption_incident_2026-09.md)**; `tests/test_scan_diagnosis.py` |
| 2026-09-05 | **R1 to R5, the first sittings of the step-by-step walk: respect is read in whole bars against the rail area, box height is graded never an exclusion, tightest wrap wins (later narrowed), and the chart is either trending or consolidating.** R1, verbatim: *"if a bar's values is within that Resistance/Support area Meaning either low or high of that bar is in that area then it counts (see the screenshot) that's why its also important to dissect the individual legs and swings and understand the patterns that the price is doing"*, and *"'A bar is outside the box only when the whole bar sits beyond the area.' correct, and after we dissect individual bars we structure and split them into Swings and build the narrative from there, because a Spring/Up Thrust's are bound to have bars that exists beyond the Resistance/Support tight area, and that is Acceptable as longs as we can detected the rest of the story. I want you to build the scanning process through visual phases that we can understand"*. R2: *"I don't know what 'Daily range mean' but my answer is that the box where the chop actually happened if most chops are at around 2-3 'Daily ranges' then we know the common ground for most consolidations that we want to find, but I don't want to exclude a perfectly 'good' consolidation just because it's taller then 3 'daily ranges'"*. R3 (Sat 05/09/2026): *"both SILC and COLM charts you sent are good"*, given on the engine's render and AMENDED Thu 10/09/2026 on the real chart (COLM is junk). R4: *"Yes lets go with the Tightest wrap Wins, since that rule will probably lend us to more high quality setups overall that can fit in a tightwrap rather then messy chops that we rather avoid"* (narrowed Tue 08/09/2026 to a same-day tie-break after the smallest-box form lost 6 pinned fires). | 2026-08-25 | **Two gates were dark, and one of them was guarding the least replaceable data in the repo.** Delegated (*"take the lead on this one"*); an engineering pass, not an operator ruling. **(1) `calibration_frames/` had NEVER been backed up.** The 2026-08-20 disposition added the frames leg to `tools/ChrolloBackup.ps1` and recorded it done — but the scheduled task runs a DEPLOYED copy at `%USERPROFILE%`, and the redeploy never ran, so the task kept executing a 2026-07-13 script with no mention of frames. It failed by ABSENCE: the leg logs either `calibration frames: N copied` OR `no calibration_frames directory yet, skipped`, and neither line appears in any run from 08-20 to 08-24 — every night logged `OK` while 110 frames (3.6 MB), each binding one of the 34 marks to the exact bars it was drawn on and **unre-derivable by design** (a live re-fetch is different bars), sat unprotected. The root cause was a fix recorded without being run, so `deploy.md` §4 now lists the frames among the irreplaceable data and names the failure mode — an un-redeployed change is inert and silent — with a run-it-and-read-the-log verification step. **(2) CI had been RED on every push since 2026-08-12** (12 consecutive failures; the Windows job passed throughout, the Ubuntu job failed 2/1,685). Both were genuine Windows-vs-Linux defects, not flake. **`frame_digest` sealed the OPERATING SYSTEM**: `to_csv` defaults `lineterminator` to `os.linesep`, so the same frame hashed differently on CRLF and LF and the battery could never pass off-Windows. Pinned to LF; **the recapture is DECLARED here per EC-29** — exactly two `frame_digest` strings in `power_play_baseline.json`, zero length delta, every read baseline byte-identical (proved by diff). Corroboration that the DATA never differed: the LF digest computed on Windows (`7518f1fc…37f3c5` for FTNT) is byte-identical to the value the Ubuntu runner reported in the failure log. The operator's marks were never at risk — they bind through `frame_store.ohlcv_digest`, a separate function that always joined on an explicit newline (the same idea implemented twice; one author got it right). **The sealed-output guard was Windows-only**: `os.path.normcase` folds case on Windows and is the identity on POSIX, so `docs/Marks/…` was refused here and waved through on Ubuntu; now casefolded explicitly. | 2026-08-24 | **The service log flood had a CAUSE, not just a missing rotation cap — found and fixed.** The 2026-08-20 disposition deleted 244.7 MB of logs and got the stderr half right (`logging` writes every level to stderr, so `chrollo-service-error.log` is HTTP request lines, not errors), but left the remedy as *"the `AppRotate*` block still needs an elevated run or it recurs"*. It recurred — 167 MB in four days. Rotation was never the remedy, because the volume itself was a defect: **`/ibkr/status` was missing from the request middleware's quiet list**, while `useIBKRStatus` polls it every 3s whenever the broker is DISCONNECTED — the permanent steady state, since boot stays broker-free by house rule. Measured: **16,698 of the last 16,850 lines (99.1%)** were that one path; the 4 tracebacks buried in there were all `KeyboardInterrupt` at service restart, so nothing real was hiding. Landed together: the path joins `_NOISY_PATH_PREFIXES`, and the streams split (INFO and below → stdout, WARNING and above → stderr) so the *error* log finally means errors. The middleware had ZERO test coverage, which is how a one-tuple omission survived four months — `tests/test_request_logging.py` now pins both contracts (a successful poll is DEBUG; a failed one stays visible). No convention row was added: per this file's own *"prefer a machine-checked invariant to a paragraph"*, the test IS the rule. The elevated `AppRotate` block is still worth running as a backstop, but it is no longer load-bearing. **Not an operator ruling — an engineering fix from a maintenance pass**, recorded here because it makes the 2026-08-20 row's remedy incomplete. Its 8-point cap is deliberately untouched: demoting a term is an edge-read decision (the `rs`/`uptrend` precedent, 1977 episodes), and no evidence was offered against a rising floor — only against grading the shakeout EVENT. **`spring` left the `ta` layer for a new third layer, `marker`**: measured, archived (`score_spring`) and drawn, excluded from `ta_layer_terms()`, so it cannot reach a chapter, the divisor or the tier even if `SCORE_SPRING` were raised. ---

## How a change to this engine should go

The order below is what actually caught mistakes, in the order it caught them.

1. **Read the theory first** ([`strategy_alpha.md`](strategy_alpha.md)), then this file's
   Tested-DEAD table. Most bad proposals die here for free.
2. **Measure before building.** State the discriminator you expect, then test it against the
   corpus *and* against the operator's marks. Four of this engine's most plausible ideas
   were nulls; finding that out costs minutes, and finding it out after implementation costs
   a day.
3. **Build it behind a default-off flag**, with a row in [`flag_ledger.md`](flag_ledger.md)
   in the same change. Flag-off must be byte-identical.
4. **A/B against the sealed marks ratchet** (`python -m tools.marks_corpus --check`) with the
   flag on. A regressed pinned hit is a design falsification, not a threshold to tune —
   diagnose the specific name before touching a constant.
5. **Run the invariant gate** (`python -m tools.doctrine_audit --check`) and the full suite.
6. **Land the docs in the same change.** Theory → `strategy_alpha.md`; implementation and
   its reasoning → `engine_reference.md`; the ruling and anything falsified → here.
7. **The flip is the operator's**, on eyeball evidence. Renders go to `tools/fidelity/`.

**Put the gotcha where the hands are.** The facts that unblocked the 2026-07-27 session came
from *docstrings* at the point of use, not from any document. If an invariant can be stated
next to the code that depends on it, state it there and let these files carry the model.

**Prefer a machine-checked invariant to a paragraph.** `tools/doctrine_audit.py` is the
highest-leverage artifact in the repo: prose tells the next session the rule, the gate proves
they did not break it. When a ruling is expressible as an invariant, add it.
